Claim 7
.

Suppose
y_{i,j}
takes the value
1
if we cut the
j
th subset from a cluster
D_{i}
in the subroutine
AlgoforMerge()
otherwise
0
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-algo}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{i,j}\,\kappa_{j}(D_{i})+y_{i,0}\,\kappa_{0}(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i})\right)+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\mu(D_{i})\leq\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*}).

Proof:
From
1
we get that if in
\mathcal{T}^{*}
,
D_{i}
gets split into
X_{i,1},\ldots,X_{i,t}
that is

•

X_{i,j}\subseteq T^{*}_{k}
(for some
T_{k}^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{*}
)
\forall j\in[t]
.

•

X_{i,j_{1}}\cap X_{i,j_{2}}=\emptyset
for
j_{1}\neq j_{2}
and
j_{1},j_{2}\in[t]
.

•

\bigcup\limits_{j\in[t]}X_{i,j}=D_{i}
.

then

1.

Either
\exists j\in[t]
, such that
X_{i,j}=T_{r_{j}}^{*}
(for some
T_{r_{j}}^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{*})
and
\textit{red}\left(D_{i}\right)\subseteq X_{i,j}
and
|X_{i,\ell}|<p

\forall\,\,\ell\neq j
.

2.

or
|X_{i,\ell}|<p

\forall\,\,\ell\in[t]
.

This implies

\left|\bigcup\limits_{\ell\neq j}X_{i,\ell}\right|\mod p=s_{i}

Hence,

\left|\bigcup\limits_{\ell\neq j}X_{i,\ell}\right|=ap+s_{i}\,\,\text{for some constant $a$.}

Let us partition the set
\textit{blue}(D_{i})
into subsets
W_{i,0},W_{i,1},\ldots,W_{i,m}
such that
m=\left(|\textit{blue}(D_{i})|-s_{i}\right)/p
where
|W_{i,0}|=s_{i}
and
|W_{i,z}|=p
for all
z>0
.

If
\left|\bigcup\limits_{\ell\neq j}X_{i,\ell}\right|=ap+s_{i}
then we say
\mathcal{T}^{*}
has cut
(a+1)
subsets
W_{i,0},W_{i,1},\ldots,W_{i,a}
from
D_{i}
.

Now since
|X_{i,\ell}|<p

\forall\ell\neq j
hence the pair
(u,v)
such that
u\in W_{i,z_{1}}
and
v\in W_{i,z_{2}}
for
z_{1}\neq z_{2}
must be counted in
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})
.

Hence, the cost of

1.

Cutting
W_{i,0}
from
D_{i}
is :
\kappa_{0}(D_{i})=s_{i}(|D_{i}|-s_{i})

2.

Cutting
W_{i,z}
from
D_{i}
for
z>0
is :
\kappa_{z}(D_{i})=p(|D_{i}|-(zp+s_{i}))
.

Recall in
\mathcal{T}^{*}
,
D_{i}
gets split into
X_{i,1},\ldots,X_{i,t}
. We say a cluster
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
also belongs to a set
cut-OPT
(say) if
t>1
; otherwise, it belongs to a set
merge-OPT
(say). Informally,
\mathcal{T}^{*}
has cut some points from the clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-OPT}
and merged some points to the clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{merge-OPT}
.

\mathcal{T}^{*}
must pay the minimum cost for cutting from the clusters that belong to
cut-OPT
and merging to the clusters in
merge-OPT
. More specifically,

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})\geq\text{min}

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-OPT}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}x_{i,z}\kappa_{z}(D_{i})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge-OPT}}\mu(D_{i})

subject to

\displaystyle x_{i,z}\leq x_{i,z^{\prime}}\,\,\text{for $z>z^{\prime}$}

(25)

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}x_{i,z}=\frac{W_{g}}{p}\,\,\text{where $W_{g}=\sum\limits_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\textit{d}(D_{i})$}

(26)

\displaystyle x_{i,z}\in\{0,1\}

(27)

Here,
m=(|\textit{blue}(D_{i})|-s_{i})/p
and
x_{i,z}
takes the value
1
if
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts the subset
W_{i,z}
from a cluster
D_{i}
otherwise
0
. Constraint
Eq.

25
denotes that if
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts a subset
W_{i,z}
then it must cut all subsets
W_{i,z^{\prime}}
such that
z^{\prime}<z
. Constraint
Eq.

26
denotes that
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts exactly
W_{g}/p
such subsets where
W_{g}
is the sum of the deficits of all clusters that belong to
merge
. Note the difference between the notations
W=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}^{\prime}}\textit{d}(D_{i})
which is used in our subroutine
AlgoforMerge()
and the notation
W_{g}=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\textit{d}(D_{i})
. Since here, we are analyzing the algorithm
AlgoforGeneral()
, we are using
W_{g}
instead of
W
.

Now, we prove two claims that explain the second constraint.

Claim 8
.

AlgoforMerge()
cuts exactly
W_{g}/p
subsets from the clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
.

Proof:
Suppose the algorithm cuts a subset
W_{i,z}
from the cluster
D_{i}
and merges it to another set of clusters
D_{m_{1}},D_{m_{2}},\ldots,D_{m_{t}}
(say).

When
AlgoforGeneral()
cuts a partition
W_{i,z}
the deficit of
D_{i}
reduces by
(p-|W_{i,z}|)
that is it gets updated to

\displaystyle\textit{d}(D_{i})=\textit{d}(D_{i})-(p-|W_{i,z}|)

(28)

and when it merges this subset to a set of clusters
D_{m_{1}},D_{m_{2}},\ldots,D_{m_{t}}
then the total deficit of clusters other than
D_{i}
reduces by
|W_{i,z}|
that is it gets updated to

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{j}\neq D_{i}}\textit{d}(D_{j})=\sum_{D_{j}\neq D_{i}}\textit{d}(D_{j})-|W_{i,z}|

(29)

Adding
Eq.

28
and
Eq.

29
, we get that the total deficit decreases exactly by
p
for cutting one part.

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{newcut}\cup\textsc{merge}^{\prime}}\textit{d}(D_{i})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{newcut}\cup\textsc{merge}^{\prime}}\textit{d}(D_{j})-p.

(30)

Hence, to decrease the deficit
W_{g}
to zero, our algorithm cuts exactly
W/p
subsets.

\jmlrQED

Claim 9
.

\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts exactly
W_{g}/p
subsets from the clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
.

Proof:
In the clustering,
\mathcal{T}^{*}
for each cluster
T_{i}\in\mathcal{T}^{*}
,
\textit{blue}(T_{i})
is divisible by
p
but in the clustering
\mathcal{D}
(input of
AlgoforGeneral()
) the total deficit is
W_{g}
.

If
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts a partition
W_{i,z}
from
D_{i}
then deficit of
D_{i}
would reduce by
(p-|W_{i,z}|)
. That is, it gets updated to

\displaystyle\textit{d}(D_{i})=\textit{d}(D_{i})-(p-|W_{i,z}|).

(31)

Now, these
|W_{i,z}|
points can reduce the total deficit of other clusters by at most
|W_{i,z}|
. That is, it gets updated to at most

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{j}\neq D_{i}}\textit{d}(D_{j})\geq\sum_{D_{j}\neq D_{i}}\textit{d}(D_{j})-|W_{i,z}|.

(32)

Adding
Eq.

31
and
Eq.

32
, we get that
\mathcal{T}^{*}
can decrease the total deficit at most by
p
by cutting one part.

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{newcut}\cup\textsc{merge}^{\prime}}\textit{d}(D_{i})\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{newcut}\cup\textsc{merge}^{\prime}}\textit{d}(D_{j})-p.

(33)

Hence, to decrease the deficit
W_{g}
to zero,
\mathcal{T}^{*}
must cut at least
W/p
subsets.

Since, by
8
, we get our algorithm can fulfill the deficit of
W_{g}
only by cutting
W_{g}/p
subsets. Since
\mathcal{T}^{*}
is the optimal clustering, it must cut at most
W_{g}/p
subsets.

Thus we get
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts exactly
W_{g}/p
subsets.

\jmlrQED

Now,

\displaystyle\text{min}\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-OPT}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}x_{i,z}\kappa_{z}(D_{i})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge-OPT}}\mu(D_{i})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\,\,

\displaystyle\text{min}\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-OPT}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}x_{i,z}\kappa_{z}(D_{i})-\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-OPT}}\mu(D_{i})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\mu(D_{i})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\,\,

\displaystyle\text{min}\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-OPT}}\left(\sum_{z=1}^{m}x_{i,z}\kappa_{z}(D_{i})+x_{i,0}\kappa_{0}(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i})\right)+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\mu(D_{i})

Now, to prove our claim

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-algo}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{i,z}\,\kappa_{j}(D_{i})+y_{i,0}\,\kappa_{0}(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i})\right)+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\mu(D_{i})\leq\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*}).

We need to prove that
x_{i,z}=1
iff
y_{i,z}=1
.

Claim 10
.

x_{i,z}=1
iff
y_{i,z}=1
.

Proof:
Let’s look at the objective function of the above ILP.

\displaystyle\text{min}\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-OPT}}\left(\sum_{z=1}^{m}x_{i,z}\kappa_{z}(D_{i})+x_{i,0}\kappa_{0}(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i})\right)+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\mu(D_{i})

and the constraint
1
of the ILP

\displaystyle x_{i,z}\leq x_{i,z^{\prime}}\,\,\text{for $z>z^{\prime}$}

The above constraint implies that if
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts a cluster
D_{i}
, it must cut the
0
th subset
W_{i,0}
of
D_{i}
. Now, to minimize the above objective function, we can assume the cost assigned to the
0
th subset of
D_{i}
is
\kappa(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i})
, (note
\kappa_{0}(D_{i})=\kappa(D_{i})
).

and the cost assigned to the
z
th subset of
D_{i}
is
\kappa_{z}(D_{i})
.

More specifically,

\displaystyle\textit{cost}(W_{i,z})=\kappa(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i})\,\,\text{for $z=0$}

\displaystyle\textit{cost}(W_{i,z})=\kappa_{z}(D_{i})\,\,\text{for $z\geq 1$}

(34)

Now, minimizing the above objective function is equivalent to minimizing the total cost of subsets that
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts.

Since, in
9
we get
\mathcal{T}^{*}
cuts exactly
W_{g}/p
subsets so to solve the above ILP exactly our algorithm needs to cut
W_{g}/p
subsets that have the minimum cost. Note that for all clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}
, we have

\displaystyle\textit{cost}(W_{i,0})

\displaystyle=(\kappa(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i}))

\displaystyle\leq(\kappa(D_{j})-\mu(D_{j}))\,\,\forall D_{j}\in\textsc{merge}

(35)

This is because

\displaystyle\textit{cost}(W_{j,0})-\textit{cost}(W_{i,0})

\displaystyle=s_{j}(|D_{j}|-s_{j})-(p-s_{j})|D_{j}|-s_{i}(|D_{i}|-s_{i})+(p-s_{i})|D_{i}|

\displaystyle=(2s_{j}-p)|D_{j}|-s_{j}^{2}+(p-2s_{i})|D_{i}|+s_{i}^{2}

(36)

\displaystyle\geq 0

Since
s_{j}=(\textit{blue}(D_{j})\mod p)\geq p/2
and
s_{i}=(\textit{blue}(D_{i})\mod p)<p/2
we get that all the terms in the
Eq.

36
are positive except the term
-s_{j}^{2}
. Now,
|D_{i}|p>s_{j}^{2}
because
p>s_{j}
, and we can assume
|D_{i}|\geq p
because if
D_{i}
is less than
p
then
\kappa(D_{i})=0
and we can ignore those clusters because we cut no points from a cluster
D_{i}
where
|D_{i}|<p
.

Hence,
\mathcal{T}^{*}
must cut the subset
W_{i,0}
from all clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}
.

Now, in the subroutine
AlgoforMerge()
at each iteration, we select the subsets
W_{i,z}
for which
\textit{cost}(W_{i,z})
is the minimum. From
8
we get
AlgoforGeneral()
also cuts
W_{g}/p
subsets which is same as
\mathcal{T}^{*}
.

Thus, our algorithm also selects the subsets that have the minimum cost and hence
x_{i,j}=1
iff
y_{i,j}=1
.

\jmlrQED

\jmlrQED

Now we prove
11
where we provide a bound on
\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})
and
\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})
paid by our algorithm
AlgoforGeneral()
. In the proof of
11
, we are going to use the following notations.

Suppose a subset
W_{i,z}
of
D_{i}
gets split into
W^{(1)}_{i,z},\ldots,W^{(t)}_{i,z}
and gets merged into clusters
D_{m_{1}},\ldots,D_{m_{t}}\in\textsc{merge}
. Let
T_{j_{1}},\ldots,T_{j_{t}}\in\mathcal{T}
be the clusters formed from
D_{m_{1}},\ldots,D_{m_{t}}
respectively by either merging deficit number of blue points to these clusters.

So, the pairs
(u,v)
such that
u\in W^{(k)}_{i,z}
and
v\in T_{j_{k}}\setminus(D_{m_{k}}\cup W^{(k)}_{i,z})
must be counted in
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T})
. Let us denote the number of such pairs by
\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})
.

Again, the pairs
u\in W^{(k_{1})}_{i,z}
and
v\in W^{(k_{2})}_{i,z}
must be counted in
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T})
. Let us denote the number of such pairs by
\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})
.

Now, let us assume

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z}).

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})

where
m=(|\textit{blue}(D_{i})|-s_{i})/p
. Now, we prove the following claim. To prove the following claim, we need to recall the following definitions

•

merge-algo
: The set of clusters
D_{m}\in\mathcal{D}
where the algorithm
AlgoforGeneral()
has merged the deficit amount of points to it.

•

cut-algo
: The set of clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
where the algorithm
AlgoforGeneral()
has cut the surplus amount of points from it.

Claim 11
.

\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})\leq 2\,\,\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})
.

Proof:
Case
1
:
When

|W_{i,z}|\leq p/2
: In this case, we prove that

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})\leq W^{(1)}_{i,z}|D_{m_{1}}|+\mu(D_{m_{2}})+\ldots+\mu(D_{m_{t-1}})+W^{(t)}_{i,z}|D_{m_{t}}|

Thus adding over all clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
and all subsets
W_{i,z}\in D_{i}
we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})

\displaystyle=\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{D_{m}\in\textsc{merge-algo}}\mu(D_{m})

\displaystyle(\text{Reason provided below)})

\displaystyle<2\,\sum_{D_{m}\in\textsc{merge-algo}}s(D_{m})(|D_{m}|-s(D_{m}))+\frac{1}{2}s(D_{m})(p-s(D_{m}))

\displaystyle<2\,\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})

\displaystyle(\text{from}\,\,\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:main-structure-of-M*})

Reason: This is because for the clusters
D_{m_{k}}
for
k\in[2,(t-1)]
the subset
W^{(k)}_{i,z}
covers the deficit of the clusters
D_{m_{k}}
fully that is
T_{j_{k}}\setminus(D_{m_{k}}\cup W^{(k)}_{i,z})=\emptyset
and for
k=1
and
k=t
we have charged only the merge cost contributed by the parts
W^{(1)}_{i,z}
and
W^{(t)}_{i,z}
respectively.

Thus, now we prove

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})\leq W^{(1)}_{i,z}|D_{m_{1}}|+\mu(D_{m_{2}})+\ldots+\mu(D_{m_{t-1}})+W^{(t)}_{i,z}|D_{m_{t}}|

Recall
\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})
is the number of pairs
(u,v)
such that
u\in W^{(k)}_{i,z}
and
v\in T_{j_{k}}\setminus(D_{m_{k}}\cup W^{(k)}_{i,z})
. Thus

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\left|W^{(k)}_{i,z}\right|\left|T_{j_{k_{1}}}\setminus(D_{m_{k_{1}}}\cup W^{(k)}_{i,z})\right|

\displaystyle<\sum_{k=1}^{t}\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\left|W^{(k)}_{i,z}\right|\dfrac{p}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\textbf{as}\,\,\left|T_{j_{k}}\setminus(D_{m_{k}}\cup W^{(k)}_{i,z})\right|<p/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\left(\left|W^{(k)}_{i,z}\right|\left|D_{m_{k}}\right|\right)

(37)

The
1/2
in the above expression comes from the fact that when we sum over all the clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
and all subsets
W_{i,z}\in D_{i}
we are counting the pairs twice once while considering the subset
W^{(k)}_{i,z}
and again while considering the subset
T_{j_{k}}\setminus(D_{m_{k}}\cup W^{(k)}_{i,z})
.

Recall
\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})
is the number of pairs
(u,v)
such that
u\in W^{(k_{1})}_{i,z}
and
v\in W^{(k_{2})}_{i,z}
. Thus

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}=1\\
k_{1}\neq k_{2}\end{subarray}}^{t}\left|W^{(k_{1})}_{i,z}\right|\left|W^{(k_{2})}_{i,z}\right|

\displaystyle<\sum_{k=1}^{t}\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\left|W^{(k)}_{i,z}\right|\dfrac{p}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\textbf{as}\,\,\left|W^{(k_{2})}_{i,z}\right|<p/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\left(\left|W^{(k)}_{i,z}\right|\left|D_{m_{k}}\right|\right)

(38)

The
1/2
in the above expression comes from the fact that when we sum over all the clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
and all subsets
W_{i,z}\in D_{i}
we are counting the pairs twice once while considering the subset
W^{(k_{1})}_{i,z}
and again while considering the subset
W^{(k_{2})}_{i,z}
.

Thus from
Eq.

37
and
Eq.

38
we get that,

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\left(\left|W^{(k)}_{i,z}\right|\left|D_{m_{k}}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=W^{(1)}_{i,z}|D_{m_{1}}|+\mu(D_{m_{2}})+\ldots+\mu(D_{m_{t-1}})+W^{(t)}_{i,z}|D_{m_{t}}|

The reason behind the last expression is as stated before, for the clusters
D_{m_{k}}
for
k\in[2,(t-1)]
the parts
W^{(k)}_{i,z}
covers the deficit of the clusters
D_{m_{k}}
entirely, that is
T_{j_{k}}\setminus\left(D_{m_{k}}\cup W^{(k)}_{i,z}\right)=\emptyset
.

Case
2
:
When

|W_{i,z}|>p/2
: For a cluster
D_{m}\in\textsc{merge-algo}
the deficit of the cluster
D_{m}
,
\textit{d}(D_{m})<p/2
. This implies the deficit of
D_{m}
is either filled by exactly one subset
W_{i,z}
or by two subsets
W_{i,z}\in D_{i}
and
W_{j,z^{\prime}}\in D_{j}
.

We divide our proof again into two subcases:

Case
2(a)
:
When
the deficit of
D_{m}
is filled by a subset
W_{i,z}\in D_{i}
: Let us assume a cluster
T_{j}\in\mathcal{T}
is created from the cluster
D_{m}\in\mathcal{D}
by merging the deficit
\textit{d}(D_{m})=d_{m}
(say) to it. Let, a part of
W_{i,z}
,
W^{(1)}_{i,z}
(say) be used to fill
d_{m}
. Hence,
\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(1)}_{i,z})=0
because the deficit is filled up by a subset that belongs to only one cluster
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
.

Again,
\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})=(|W^{(1)}_{i,z}|\,\,(|W_{i,z}|-|W^{(1)}_{i,z}|)\,)
because when we merge
W^{(1)}_{i,z}
part of
W_{i,z}
to
D_{m}
and the remaining part
(W_{i,z}-W^{(1)}_{i,z})
is merged to other clusters in
merge-algo
. Hence,

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(1)}_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})

\displaystyle=|W^{(1)}_{i,z}|\,\,(|W_{i,z}|-|W^{(1)}_{i,z}|)

\displaystyle\leq(d_{m}\cdot(p-d_{m}))

\displaystyle(\because|W^{(1)}_{i,z}|=d_{m}\,\,\text{and}\,\,|W_{i,z}|\leq p)

From
Lemma

24
we get that for a cluster
D_{m}\in\textsc{merge-algo}

\displaystyle\textsc{OPT}_{D_{m}}

\displaystyle\geq\textit{s}{(D_{m})}(|D_{m}|-\textit{s}{(D_{m})})+\frac{1}{2}\textit{s}{(D_{m})}(p-\textit{s}{(D_{m})})

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{2}d_{m}\,\,(p-d_{m})

\displaystyle(\because|D_{m}|>(p-d_{m}))

(40)

Hence, by
Section

C.2.2
and
Eq.

40
we get, that we can charge the
2\,\,\textsc{OPT}_{D_{m}}
for
\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(1)}_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})
.

Case
2(b)
:
When
deficit of the cluster
\textit{d}(D_{m})=d_{m}
is filled by the subsets of exactly two clusters
W_{i,z}\in D_{i}
and
W_{j,z}\in D_{j}
: Let us assume a cluster
T_{j}\in\mathcal{T}
is created from the cluster
D_{m}
by merging the deficit
\textit{d}(D_{m})=d_{m}
(say) to it. Let, a part of
W_{i,z}
,
W^{(2)}_{i,z}
is used to fill the deficit of
D_{m}
. Similarly, let part of
W_{j,z}
,
W^{(1)}_{j,z}
is used to fill the deficit of
D_{m}
. Hence,

\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(2)}_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(1)}_{j,z})=|W^{(2)}_{i,z}|\,\,|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|

and

\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{j,z})=|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|\,\,(|W_{j,z}|-|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|)

( note we have already charged the cost
\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})
in case
1
).

Hence,

\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(2)}_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(1)}_{j,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{j,z})=(|W^{(1)}_{i,z}|\,\,|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|)+(|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|\,\,(|W_{j,z}|-|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|)).

Now, WLOG we can assume
(|W_{j,z}|-|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|)<p/2

because otherwise there exists a portion
W^{(1^{\prime})}_{j,z}
of
(W_{j,z}\setminus W^{(1)}_{j,z})
that is used to fill the deficit of some cluster fully in
merge-algo
and thus we have charged
\textit{cost}_{4}(w_{j,z})
in case
(1)
.

Hence,

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(2)}_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(1)}_{j,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{j,z})

\displaystyle=(|W^{(2)}_{i,z}|\,\,|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|)+(|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|\,\,(|W_{j,z}|-|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|))

\displaystyle\leq|W^{(2)}_{i,z}|\,\,d_{m}+|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|\,\,p/2

\displaystyle(\textbf{as}\,\,|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|<d_{m}\,\text{and}\,(|W_{j,z}|-|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|)<p/2)

\displaystyle\leq(|W^{(2)}_{i,z}|+|W^{(1)}_{j,z}|)\,\,(p-d_{m})

\displaystyle(\textbf{as}\,\,d_{m}\,\,\text{and}\,\,p/2\leq(p-d_{m}))

\displaystyle=d_{m}\,\,(p-d_{m})

(41)

Hence, by
Eq.

41
,
Eq.

40
and
Lemma

24
we get, that we can charge the
2\,\,\textsc{OPT}_{D_{m}}
for
\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(2)}_{i,z})+\textit{cost}_{3}(W^{(1)}_{j,z})+\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{j,z})
.

Since,

\displaystyle d_{m}(p-d_{m})

\displaystyle=(p-s(D_{m}))s(D_{m})

\displaystyle<2\,(s(D_{m})(|D_{m}|-s(D_{m}))+\frac{1}{2}s(D_{m})(p-s(D_{m})))

\displaystyle<2\textsc{OPT}_{D_{m}}

Thus, combining the above two cases, we get,

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}\textit{cost}_{3}(W_{i,z})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{z=0}^{m}\textit{cost}_{4}(W_{i,z})

\displaystyle=\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\,\sum_{D_{m}\in\textsc{merge-algo}}\textsc{OPT}_{D_{m}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})

\displaystyle(\text{from}\,\,\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:main-structure-of-M*})

\jmlrQED

Now we are ready to prove
Lemma

22

Proof of
Lemma

22
:

Suppose
y_{i,j}
takes the value
1
if we cut the
j
th subset of size
p
from a cluster
D_{i}
in the subroutine
AlgoforMerge()
otherwise
0
. Hence,

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-algo}}\sum_{j=0}^{n}y_{i,j}\kappa_{i}(D_{j})+\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{merge-algo}}\mu(D_{j})+\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T}).

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-algo}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{i,j}\,\kappa_{j}(D_{i})+y_{i,0}\,\kappa_{0}(D_{i})-\mu(D_{i})\right)+\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\mu(D_{i})+\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T}).

\displaystyle\leq\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})+2\,\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*}).

\displaystyle=3\,\,\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*}).

\jmlrQED

C.2.3
Approximation guarantee in the cut case

In this section, we show that, in the cut case, the clustering output by our algorithm
AlgoforGeneral()
has a distance of at most
3.5\textsc{OPT}
from the input clustering. In particular, we prove the following lemma that we restate below.
See
23

To prove
Lemma

23
, we need to define and recall some definitions.

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T}):\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}s_{i}(|D_{i}|-s_{i})

(42)

It is the total cost of cutting the surplus blue points from a cluster
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
.

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T}):\sum_{D_{j}\in\mathcal{D}}(p-s_{j})|D_{j}|

(43)

It is the total cost of merging the
\textit{d}(D_{j})
number of blue points to a cluster
D_{j}\in\mathcal{D}
.

Now, let’s recall the following definitions

where for a cluster
T_{k}\in\mathcal{T}
we defined
\pi(T_{k})
as a parent of
T_{k}
iff
\pi(T_{k})\in\mathcal{D}
and
T_{k}
is formed either by cutting some points from
\pi(T_{k})
or by merging some points to
\pi(T_{k})
.

Thus, our algorithm pays the above four types of costs.

To prove
Lemma

23
, first, we need to prove some claims.

We first argue (in
12
) that
\mathcal{T}^{*}
must pay
\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\frac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}
in the cut case where
s_{i}=\textit{s}{(D_{i})}
.

Claim 12
.

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}

Proof:
Suppose there is an input cluster
D_{k}\in\mathcal{D}
that is split into
t
parts (say)
X_{k,1},\ldots,X_{k,t}
.

We can assume that the cluster
D_{k}
consists only of blue points. If it contains red points, the cost paid by
\mathcal{T}^{*}
for splitting
D_{k}
into
t
parts would be more because the red points would increase the size of the cluster
D_{k}
. Let us assume that the surplus of
X_{k,i}
be
s_{k,j}
and to make the number of blue points in each cluster a multiple of
p

\mathcal{T}^{*}
must merge at least
(p-s_{k,j})
blue points to
X_{k,j}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\textsc{OPT}_{D_{k}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{t}s_{k,j}(p-s_{k,j})\right)

(44)

Now, since
\sum_{j=1}^{t}s_{k,j}\mod p=s_{k}
thus from
Proposition

25
we get,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{t}s_{k,j}(p-s_{k,j})\right)\geq\frac{s_{k}(p-s_{k})}{2}

(45)

Now,

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\textsc{OPT}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\frac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}

\displaystyle(\text{from}\,\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{equn:one}\,\text{and}\,\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{equn:two})

This completes the proof.

\jmlrQED

The following lemma is a consequence of
12

Claim 13
.

\displaystyle\textsc{OPT}\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}

Here,
s_{i}=\textit{s}{(D_{i})}
and
d_{i}=\textit{d}(D_{i})
.

Proof:
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}.

For each
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}
,
s_{i}\leq\frac{p}{2}
. Hence
s_{i}(p-s_{i})\geq s_{i}\frac{p}{2}\geq s_{i}^{2}
.

For each
D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}
,
s_{i}\geq\frac{p}{2}
. Hence
s_{i}(p-s_{i})\geq\frac{p}{2}(p-s_{i})\geq d_{i}^{2}
(where
d_{i}=(p-s_{i})
).

These observations imply that

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}\leq\textsc{OPT},

where the last inequality is from
12
.

This completes the proof.

\jmlrQED

Claim 14
.

If
\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}}\textit{s}{(D_{i})}>\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{merge}}\textit{d}(D_{j})
then
\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T})\leq 2\,\textsc{OPT}
.

Proof:
By the definition of
\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})
, we have
\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-algo}}\kappa(D_{i})
. Now, if
\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}}\textit{s}{(D_{i})}>\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{merge}}\textit{d}(D_{j})
that is in the cut case we have
\textsc{cut-algo}=\textsc{cut}
. Recall that
cut-algo
is the set of clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
from which we cut some points at any point during the execution of our algorithm
AlgoforGeneral()
. Since in the cut case, we only cut from the clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}
that is the clusters
D_{i}
for which
\textit{s}{(D_{i})}\leq p/2
we have
\textsc{cut-algo}=\textsc{cut}
. Hence, in the cut case, we have,

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut-algo}}\kappa(D_{i})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}}\kappa(D_{i}).

(46)

Similarly, in the cut case, we also have
\textsc{merge-algo}=\textsc{merge}
thus we have

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge-algo}}\mu(D_{i})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\mu(D_{i}).

(47)

Hence, from
Eq.

46
and
Eq.

47
we have

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T})=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}}\kappa(D_{i})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\mu(D_{i}).

(48)

Now, from
Lemma

24
we get for all clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}
we have

\displaystyle\textsc{OPT}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle\geq s(D_{i})(|D_{i}|-s(D_{i}))+\frac{1}{2}s(D_{i})(p-s(D_{i}))

\displaystyle>\kappa(D_{i})

and for all clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}
we have

\displaystyle\textsc{OPT}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle\geq(p-s(D_{i}))(|D_{i}|-s(D_{i}))+\frac{1}{2}s(D_{i})(p-s(D_{i}))

\displaystyle\geq 2\,\mu(D_{i})

Thus we have

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}}\textsc{OPT}_{D_{i}}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\textsc{OPT}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}}\kappa(D_{i})+2\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\mu(D_{i}).

(49)

Now, from
Eq.

49
and
Eq.

48
we get

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\,\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{T}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\,\textsc{OPT}.

\jmlrQED

Claim 15
.

\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})\leq 1.5\,\textsc{OPT}
.

Proof:

We introduce some notations.

For each cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}
, denote by
X_{i,{1}},X_{i,{2}},\dots,X_{i,{\ell_{i}}}
the partition of
s_{i}
blue points that are split from
D_{i}
, and each of these parts are merged to different clusters
D_{j}
’s in
merge
, for
k=1,2,\dots,\ell_{j}
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of clusters of size
p
formed by
AlgoforCut()
. Let
E_{j}
be its
j
th cluster. Represents
E_{j}=Y_{j,1}\cup Y_{j,2}\cup\dots\cup Y_{j,y_{j}}
where each
Y_{j,k}
is a set of blue points coming from a cluster
D_{j_{k}}\in\textsc{cut}
.

We prove this claim by showing the correctness of the following claims.

Claim 16
.

\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}{\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\right)}.

Claim 17
.

\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{y_{j}}|Y_{j,k}|(p-|Y_{j,k}|)\right).

Claim 18
.

\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}{\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\right)}+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{y_{j}}|Y_{j,k}|(p-|Y_{j,k}|)\right)\leq\dfrac{3}{2}\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}.

Given the correctness of the claims above, the lemma can be proved as follows.

\displaystyle\>\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\>+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\right)+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{y_{j}}|Y_{j,k}|(p-|Y_{j,k}|)\right)

\displaystyle(\text{by~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{clm:cost 4} and~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{clm:cost 3}})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}+\dfrac{3}{2}\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}

\displaystyle(\text{by~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{clm:three half cost}})

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{3}{2}\left(\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{3}{2}\textsc{OPT}.

\displaystyle(\text{by~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem.opt.si.times.p.min.si.breakdown}})

We now proceed to the proof of each of the above claims to finish the proof:

Proof for
16
:

By definition,

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}}{\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j,}||X_{i,k}|\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}{\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\right)}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}{\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\right)}

Note that
\sum_{1\leq j\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}|=s_{i}
, therefore
\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{1\leq j\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}|\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}\setminus\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{2}s_{i}^{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}{\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\right)}.

\jmlrQED

Proof for
17
:

For each cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}
,
AlgoforGeneral()
brings
d_{i}
blue points from some other clusters
D_{i^{\prime}}\in\textsc{cut}
into
D_{i}
. Denote by
Y_{i,1},Y_{i,2},\dots,Y_{i,y_{i}}
the partition of these
d_{i}
blue points such that in the following order,
AlgoforGeneral()
merges into
D_{i}
:
Y_{i,1}
from
D_{i_{1}}
,
Y_{i,2}
from
D_{i_{2}}
, and so on,
Y_{i,y_{i}}
from
D_{i_{y_{i}}}
. This implies

\displaystyle d_{i}=|Y_{i,1}|+|Y_{i,2}|+\dots+|Y_{i,y_{i}}|,

(50)

and the cost contributed to
\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})
by this cluster
D_{i}
is

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{y_{i}}|Y_{i,j}|(d_{i}-|Y_{i,j}|)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2y_{i}}d_{i}(y_{i}-1)d_{i}

(51)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}.

(52)

To obtain (
51
), we use Chebyshev inequality (
\frac{a_{1}b_{1}+a_{2}b_{2}+\dots a_{n}b_{n}}{n}\leq\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}+\dots a_{n}}{n}\frac{b_{1}+b_{2}+\dots b_{n}}{n}
, for
a_{1}\leq a_{2}\leq\dots\leq a_{n},b_{1}\geq b_{2}\geq\dots\geq b_{n}
), combining with (
50
).

Similarly, for each cluster of size
p
,
E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}
, we can assume
E_{j}
is formed by
Y_{j,1}
from
D_{j_{1}}
,
Y_{j,2}
from
D_{j_{2}}
, and so on,
Y_{j,{y_{j}}}
from
D_{j_{y_{j}}}
. Therefore, the cost contributed to
\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})
by
P_{j}
is

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{y_{j}}|Y_{j,k}|(p-|Y_{j,k}|)

(53)

From (
52
) and (
53
), we have

\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{merge}}\dfrac{1}{2}d_{i}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{y_{j}}|Y_{j,k}|(p-|Y_{j,k}|)\right).

\jmlrQED

Proof for
18
:

Let

A=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}{\left(\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|\right)}+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{y_{j}}|Y_{j,k}|(p-|Y_{j,k}|)\right),

and
A^{\prime}=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{i}(p-s_{i})}{2}
. We need to show that
A\leq\dfrac{3}{2}A^{\prime}
.

In each cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}
,
AlgoforCut()
splits
s_{i}
blue points from it, and these
s_{i}
blue points are split again into at most two parts. Therefore, we can write
\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq\ell_{i}}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,k}|=|X_{i,1}|(s_{i}-|X_{i,1}|)=\alpha_{i,1}(s_{i}-\alpha_{i,1})
, where
0<\alpha_{i,1}=|X_{i,1}|\leq s_{i}
.

Each cluster
E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}
is formed by merging
Y_{j,1}
from
D_{j_{1}}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}
,
Y_{j,2}
from
D_{j_{2}}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}
, and so on,
Y_{j,{y_{j}}}
from
D_{j_{y_{j}}}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}
, in that order. Therefore,
|Y_{j,k}|=s_{j_{k}}
for
2\leq k<y_{j}
, and
|Y_{j,1}|=\alpha_{j_{1}}\leq s_{j_{1}}
,
|Y_{j,y_{j}}|=\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}\leq s_{j_{y_{j}}}
.

A
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\alpha_{i,1}(s_{i}-\alpha_{i,1})

\displaystyle\>+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\alpha_{j_{1}}(p-\alpha_{j_{1}})+\sum_{t=2}^{y_{j}-1}s_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(p-s_{j^{\prime}_{t}})+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}(p-\beta_{j_{y_{j}}})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\alpha_{j_{1}}(p-\alpha_{j_{1}})+\sum_{t=2}^{y_{j}-1}s_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(p-s_{j^{\prime}_{t}})+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}(p-\beta_{j_{y_{j}}})\right)+\alpha_{j_{1}}(s_{j_{1}}-\alpha_{j_{1}})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\Bigl(\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\alpha_{j_{1}}(p-s_{j_{1}})+\sum_{t=2}^{y_{j}-1}s_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(p-s_{j^{\prime}_{t}})+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}(p-s_{j_{y_{j}}})\right)

\displaystyle\>+3\alpha_{j_{1}}(s_{j_{1}}-\alpha_{j_{1}})+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}(s_{j_{y_{j}}}-\beta_{j_{y_{j}}})\Bigl)

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}}\dfrac{s_{j}(p-s_{j})}{2}+\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(3\alpha_{j_{1}}(s_{j_{1}}-\alpha_{j_{1}})+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}(s_{j_{y_{j}}}-\beta_{j_{y_{j}}})\right)

\displaystyle=A^{\prime}+\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\dfrac{1}{2}(3\alpha_{j_{1}}(s_{j_{1}}-\alpha_{j_{1}})+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}(s_{j_{y_{j}}}-\beta_{j_{y_{j}}})).

Since
s_{j}\leq\frac{p}{2}
for all
D_{j}\in\textsc{cut}
, and
p=\alpha_{j_{1}}+s_{j_{2}}+\dots+s_{j_{y_{j}}}+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle A^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{E_{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\dfrac{1}{2}(\alpha_{j_{1}}+s_{j_{2}}+\dots+s_{j_{y_{j}}}+\beta_{j_{y_{j}}})\dfrac{p}{2}

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{E}|\dfrac{p^{2}}{4}.

Moreover,
\alpha_{j_{1}}(s_{j_{1}}-\alpha_{j_{1}})\leq\dfrac{s_{j_{1}}^{2}}{4}\leq\dfrac{p^{2}}{16}
, and
\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}(s_{j_{y_{j}}}-\beta_{j_{y_{j}}}))\leq\dfrac{s_{j_{y_{j}}}^{2}}{4}\leq\dfrac{p^{2}}{16}
. Combining these facts, we get

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq A^{\prime}+|\mathcal{E}|\dfrac{1}{2}\left(3\dfrac{p^{2}}{16}+\dfrac{p^{2}}{16}\right)

\displaystyle=A^{\prime}+|\mathcal{E}|\dfrac{p^{2}}{8}\leq\dfrac{3}{2}A^{\prime},

which is desired.

\jmlrQED

\jmlrQED

Now we are ready to prove
Lemma

23
.

Proof of
Lemma

23
:

Let
\mathcal{T}
be the output of
AlgoforGeneral()
in the cut case. Let
\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T}),\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T}),

\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T}),

\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})
be the four costs that are paid by our algorithm when in
AlgoforGeneral()
, we are left with the clusters present only in
cut
.
Lemma

23
is essentially showing that

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})\leq 2\,\textsc{OPT}+1.5\textsc{OPT}.

We complete the proof by applying
14
, which gives

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{T})\leq 2\,\textsc{OPT},

and applying
15
, which gives

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{T})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{T})\leq 1.5\textsc{OPT}.

\jmlrQED

Hence, from
14
and
15
we get that
\mathcal{T}^{*}
pays at most
3.5\,\,\textsc{OPT}
in the cut case.

Appendix D
Closest Balanced Clustering for
p,q>1

In this section, given a clustering
\mathcal{D}=\{D_{1},D_{2},\ldots,D_{\phi}\}
of a set of red-blue colored points, where the irreducible ratio of blue to red points is
p/q
for some integer
p,q>1
, it returns a balanced clustering
\mathcal{Q}
.
That is, in the resulting clustering
\mathcal{Q}
, every

Q_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}
, satisfies the condition that
|\textit{red}\left(Q_{i}\right)|
is a multiple of
q
and
|\textit{blue}(Q_{i})|
is a multiple of
p
. Our main result for this section is the following:

See
21

We first provide an algorithm to find a
7.5
-close
Balanced clustering
of
\mathcal{D}
and then analyze the algorithm in the subsequent subsections.

D.1
Details of the Algorithm

Before describing our algorithm, let us first define a few notations that we use later.

For any cluster, if its number of red (or blue) points is not a multiple of
q
(or
p
), then we define its red (or blue) surplus as the minimum number of red( or blue) points whose removal makes the number of red (or blue) points to be a multiple of
q
(or
p
), and we define its deficit as the minimum number of red (or blue) points whose addition makes the number of red (or blue) points to be a multiple of
q
(or
p
). Formally, we define them as follows.

•

Red Surplus of
D_{i}
,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
: For any cluster
D_{i}\subseteq V
,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}=|\textit{red}\left(D_{i}\right)|\mod q
.

•

Blue Surplus of
D_{i}
,
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
: For any cluster
D_{i}\subseteq V
,
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}=|\textit{blue}(D_{i})|\mod p
.

•

Red Deficit of
D_{i}
,
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})
: For any cluster
D_{i}\subseteq V
if
q\nmid|\textit{red}\left(D_{i}\right)|
then
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})=q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
otherwise
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})=0
.

•

Blue Deficit of
D_{i}
,
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})
: For any cluster
D_{i}\subseteq V
if
p\nmid|\textit{blue}(D_{i})|
then
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})=p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
otherwise
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})=0
.

Based on the surplus of a cluster, we define four sets of clusters as follows.

•

\textsc{RedCut}=\{D_{i}\ |\ \textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\leq q/2\}
;

•

\textsc{RedMerge}=\{D_{i}\ |\ \textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}>q/2\}
;

•

\textsc{BlueCut}=\{D_{i}\ |\ \textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}\leq p/2\}
;

•

\textsc{BlueMerge}=\{D_{i}\ |\ \textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}>p/2\}
.

A cluster belongs to exactly two sets. For example, a cluster with a red surplus greater than
q/2
and a blue surplus smaller than
p/2
, that is,
s_{b}(D_{i})\leq p/2
and
s_{r}(D_{i})>p/2
. Such clusters belong to
BlueCut
and
RedMerge
. Therefore, the input clustering is a partition (union of disjoint sets)
\mathcal{D}=(\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut})\cup(\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge})\cup(\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut})\cup(\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge})
.

We briefly describe our algorithm to find a
7.5
-close
Balanced clustering
of
\mathcal{D}
.

Description of the algorithm

Our algorithm
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
(
Section

D.1
) aims to, for each cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}
, cut
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points from
D_{i}
and merge these
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points to some clusters
D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
. Similarly,
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
blue points are cut from a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}
and merged to some clusters
D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}
.

We describe the implementation in
Section

D.1
. We initialize the sets
\textsc{RCut}=\textsc{RedCut}
,
\textsc{RMerge}=\textsc{RedMerge}
,
\textsc{BlueCut}=\textsc{BCut}
, and
\textsc{BMerge}=\textsc{BlueMerge}
. Moreover, in
RCut
and
BCut
, we discard the sets
D_{i},D_{j}
with
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}=0
and
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})}=0
, respectively. The clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
are sorted in non-increasing order based on
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})-\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})|D_{i}|
. The algorithm iterates through each cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}
. For each such
D_{i}
, the algorithm takes a cluster
D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
, and transfer at most
\min(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})},\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{j}))
red points from
D_{i}
to
D_{j}
. After this, the number of red points in
D_{i}
or
D_{j}
must be a multiple of
q
. If
q||\textit{red}\left(D_{i}\right)|
, the algorithm iterates to the next cluster in
RedCut
. Otherwise, if
q||\textit{red}\left(D_{j}\right)|
, the algorithm finds another cluster in
RedMerge
and repeats transferring red points from
D_{i}
into this one. This process proceeds until
RCut
or
RMerge
becomes empty. A similar process is also applied for
BlueCut
and
BlueMerge
.

After this, we will be in only one of four cases, which will be defined below. These four cases can be easily identified by the input clustering
\mathcal{D}
.

•

Cut-Cut case:
If all the sets are empty or if the nonempty set of clusters is
RCut
or
BCut
, or both
RCut
and
BCut
. This case occurs if the following holds

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\geq\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})})

and

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}\geq\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})})

•

Cut-Merge case:
If the remaining sets of clusters are
RCut
and
BMerge
. This case occurs when the following holds

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}>\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})})

and

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}<\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})})

•

Merge-Cut case:
If the remaining sets of clusters are
RMerge
and
BCut
. This case occurs when the following holds

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}<\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})})

and

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}>\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})})

•

Merge-Merge case:
If the remaining set of cluster is
RMerge
, or
BMerge
, or both
RMerge
and
BMerge
. This case occurs when the following holds.

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\leq\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})})

and

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}\leq\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})})

Each of these cases is handled by using specific subroutines. We provide a brief description of all the subroutines below.

AlgoforCut-Cut()
(
Section

D.1
): As mentioned previously we call the subroutine
AlgoforCut-Cut()
in the cut-cut case, that is when the remaining set of clusters
D_{i}
belongs to
RedCut
or
BlueCut
, or both
RedCut
and
BlueCut
. In this case, we cut
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points from every remaining cluster
D_{i}
and form extra clusters of size
q
, and we also cut
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
blue points from every remaining cluster
D_{i}
and form extra clusters of size
p
. We do this operation sequentially until, in every cluster, the number of red points is a multiple of
q
and the number of blue points is a multiple of
p
.

AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
) : We call the subroutine
AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
) in the cut-merge case when the remaining set of clusters
D_{i}
belong to
RedCut
and
BlueMerge
. In this case, on the red side, we cut
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points from every cluster
D_{i}
and form extra clusters of size
q
. On the blue side, since the surplus
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}>p/2
, we want to merge the deficit amount of blue points in these clusters. This algorithm is a bit more involved and we describe it below.

AlgoforGeneralPQ()
takes a clustering
\mathcal{D}
as input where the clusters
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
can belong to any of the four sets
RedCut
,
BlueCut
,
RedMerge
or
BlueMerge
. The Cut-Merge case occurs when the total surplus of the clusters belonging to
BlueCut
is less than the total deficit of the clusters belonging to
BlueMerge
, that is

\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}<\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}}\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i}).

Before the subroutine
AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
) is called, the algorithm
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
cuts the blue surplus points from the clusters in
BlueCut
and merges these blue points to the clusters in
BlueMerge
. At this point, for all clusters in
BlueCut
, the number of blue points is a multiple of
p
. To remove any ambiguity, let us call this transformed set of clusters
newbcut
and the remaining set of clusters in
BlueMerge
where the number of blue points is still not a multiple of
p
as
\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
.

The subroutine
AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
) takes two sets
\textsc{newbcut},\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
as input. In
AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
), we calculate the sum of
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})
for each of the remaining clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
. Let,
W_{b}=\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}}\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})
. Now, each cluster
D_{k}\in\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
the set
\textit{blue}(D_{k})
is divided into
(|\textit{blue}(D_{k})|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})})/p
subsets of size
p
and one subset of size
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})}
. Let us number the subset of size
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})}
as
0
, and all other subsets of size
p
are numbered arbitrarily.

Each of these subsets is attached with a cost

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}(D_{j})=\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})}\left(|D_{j}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})}-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})}\right)

cutting cost for
0
th subset

\displaystyle\kappa_{z}(D_{j})=p\left(|D_{j}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})}-(zp+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})})\right)

cutting cost for
z
th subset of size
p
where
z\neq 0

Now, in the algorithm
AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
), we cut
W_{b}/p
blue subsets with the minimum cost. Let us call the set of clusters from where we have cut at least one subset as
\mathcal{A}
. We merge these subsets into the clusters present in
\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{A}
.

AlgoforMerge-Cut()
(
Section

D.1
): The description of the algorithm
AlgoforMerge-Cut()
is similar to the description of algorithm
AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
).

AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
): The description of the algorithm
AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
) is again similar to the description of the merge part in the algorithm
AlgoforCut-Merge()
(
Section

D.1
). Before the subroutine
AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
) is called, the algorithm
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
cuts the blue surplus points from the clusters in
BlueCut
and merges these blue points to the clusters in
BlueMerge
. At this point, for all clusters in
BlueCut
, the number of blue points is a multiple of
p
. To remove any ambiguity, let us call this transformed set of clusters
newbcut
and the remaining set of clusters in
BlueMerge
where the number of blue points is still not a multiple of
p
as
\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
. Similarly, on the red side, let us call the transformed set of clusters from
RedCut
after the algorithm
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
be
newrcut
and the remaining set of clusters in
RedMerge
where the number of red points is still not a multiple of
q
as
\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
.

The subroutine
AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
) takes four sets
\textsc{newbcut},

\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime},

\textsc{newrcut},

\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
as input. First, in
AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
), we calculate the sum of
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})
for each of the remaining clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
. Let,
W_{b}=\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}}\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})
. Now, each cluster
D_{k}\in\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
the set
\textit{blue}(D_{k})
is divided into
(|\textit{blue}(D_{k})|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})})/p
subsets of size
p
and one subset of size
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})}
. Let us number the subset of size
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})}
as
0
, and all other subsets of size
p
are numbered arbitrarily.

Each of these subsets is attached with a cost

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}(D_{j})=\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})}\left(|D_{j}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})}\right)

cutting cost for
0
th subset

\displaystyle\kappa_{z}(D_{j})=p\left(|D_{j}|-(zp+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})})\right)

cutting cost for
z
th subset of size
p
where
z\neq 0

Now, in the algorithm
AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
), we cut
W_{b}/p
blue subsets with the minimum cost. Let us call the set of clusters from where we have cut at least one subset as
\mathcal{A}_{b}
. We merge these blue subsets of size
p
into the clusters present in
\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{b}
.

After that,
AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
) does the same thing on the red side. Here, we calculate the sum of
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})
for each of the remaining clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
. Let,
W_{r}=\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}}\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{j})
. Now, for each cluster
D_{k}\in\textsc{newrcut}\cup\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
the set
\textit{red}\left(D_{k}\right)
is divided into
(|\textit{red}\left(D_{k}\right)|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{k})})/q
subsets of size
q
and one subset of size
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{k})}
. Let us number the subset of size
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{k})}
as
0
, and all other subsets of size
q
are numbered arbitrarily.

Each of these subsets is attached with a cost

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}(D_{j})=\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})}\left(|D_{j}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})}\right)

cutting cost for
0
th subset

\displaystyle\kappa_{z}(D_{j})=p\left(|D_{j}|-(zp+\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})})\right)

cutting cost for
z
th subset of size
p
where
z\neq 0

Now, in the algorithm
AlgoforMerge-Merge
(
Section

D.1
), we cut
W_{r}/q
red subsets with the minimum cost. Let us call the set of clusters from where we have cut at least one subset as
\mathcal{A}_{r}
. We merge these blue subsets of size
p
into the clusters present in
\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{r}
.

Runtime Analysis of
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
: Executing the algorithm
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
is equivalent to executing the algorithm
AlgoforGeneral()
(
Section

C.1
) in case of
p>1,q=1
twice – once to make the red points in every cluster a multiple of
q
and again to make the number of blue points in every cluster a multiple of
p
.

When we make the number of red points in every cluster a multiple of
q
, we need to sort the clusters in
RedMerge
; this takes
O(|\textsc{RedMerge}|\log(\textsc{RedMerge}))=O(n\log n)
time and again to make the number of blue points in every cluster a multiple of
p
we need to sort the clusters in
BlueMerge
this would again take
O(|\textsc{BlueMerge}|\log(\textsc{BlueMerge}))=O(n\log n)
time.

In this algorithm, a point
v\in V
is used at most twice – once while cutting and again while merging. Hence, the cutting and merging process takes at most
O(n)
time. Thus, the total time complexity is
O(n\log n)
.

{algorithm2e}
\SetAlgoVlined
\DontPrintSemicolon

AlgoforGeneralPQ()
\KwData
Input set of clusters
\mathcal{D}

\KwResult
A set of clusters
\mathcal{Q}
, such that in each cluster
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}
the number of blue points is a multiple of
p
, and the number of red points is a multiple of
q
.

\textsc{RCut}\leftarrow\{D_{i}\ |\ 0<\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\leq q/2\}
and
\textsc{newrcut}\leftarrow\{D_{i}|\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}=0\}

\textsc{RMerge}\leftarrow D_{i}\ |\ \textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\leq q/2\}

\textsc{BCut}\leftarrow\{D_{i}\ |\ 0<\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}\leq p/2\}
and
\textsc{newbcut}\leftarrow\{D_{i}|\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}=0\}

\textsc{BMerge}\leftarrow\{D_{i}\ |\ \textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}\leq q/2\}

Q^{cm}\leftarrow\{D_{i}|\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}=\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}=0\}

Sort the clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
based on their
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})-\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})|D_{i}|
in non-increasing order.

\While

\textsc{RCut}\neq\emptyset
and
\textsc{RMerge}\neq\emptyset

\For
D_{i}\in\textsc{RCut}

\For
D_{j}\in\textsc{RMerge}

k\leftarrow\min(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})},\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{j}))

Cut a set of
k
red points from
D_{i}
and merge to
D_{j}

\uIf
k=\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{j})

\textsc{RMerge}=\textsc{RMerge}\setminus\{D_{j}\}

\lIf

\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{j})}=0

Q^{cm}=Q^{cm}\cup D_{j}

\uIf

k=\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}

\textsc{RCut}\leftarrow\textsc{RCut}\setminus\{D_{i}\}
,
\textsc{newrcut}\leftarrow\textsc{newrcut}\cup\{D_{i}\}

\lIf

\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}=0

Q^{cm}=Q^{cm}\cup D_{i}

Break

Sort the clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}
based on their
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})-\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})|D_{i}|
in non-increasing order.

\While
\textsc{BCut}\neq\emptyset
and
\textsc{BMerge}\neq\emptyset

\For
D_{i}\in\textsc{BCut}

\For
D_{j}\in\textsc{BMerge}

k\leftarrow\min(\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})},\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j}))

Cut a set of
k
blue points from
D_{i}
and merge to
D_{j}

\uIf
k=\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})

\textsc{BMerge}\leftarrow\textsc{BMerge}\setminus\{D_{j}\}

\lIf

\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})}=0

Q^{cm}=Q^{cm}\cup D_{j}

\uIf
k=\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}

\textsc{BCut}\leftarrow\textsc{BCut}\setminus\{D_{i}\}
,
\textsc{newbcut}\leftarrow\textsc{newbcut}\cup\{D_{i}\}

\lIf

\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}=0

Q^{cm}=Q^{cm}\cup D_{i}

Break

\uIf
\textsc{RMerge}=\emptyset
and
\textsc{BMerge}=\emptyset

\Return
Q^{cm}\cup\text{AlgoforCut-Cut()}(\textsc{RCut},\textsc{BCut})

\tcp
Section

D.1

\uElseIf
\textsc{RMerge}=\emptyset
and
\textsc{BCut}=\emptyset

\Return
Q^{cm}\cup\text{AlgoforCut-Merge()}(\textsc{RCut},\textsc{newbcut},\textsc{BMerge})

\tcp
Section

D.1

\uElseIf

\textsc{RCut}=\emptyset
and
\textsc{BMerge}=\emptyset

\Return
Q^{cm}\cup\text{AlgoforMerge-Cut()}(\textsc{newrcut},\textsc{RMerge},\textsc{BCut})

\tcp
Section

D.1

\Else
\tcc
Section

D.1

\Return
Q^{cm}\cup\text{AlgoforMerge-Merge()}(\textsc{newrcut},\textsc{RMerge},\textsc{newbcut},\textsc{BMerge})

{algorithm2e}
\DontPrintSemicolon

\text{AlgoforCut-Cut()}(\textsc{RCut},\textsc{BCut})
\KwData
RCut
: set of cluster
C_{i}
with the red surplus
0<\textit{$s_{r}$}{(C_{i})}\leq p/2
;

BCut
: set of clusters with the blue surplus
0<\textit{$s_{b}$}{(C_{i})}\leq p/2
.

\KwResult
Set of clusters
\mathcal{Q}^{cc}
, such that in each cluster
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}^{cc}
the number of blue points is a multiple of
p
and the number of red points is a multiple of
q
.

m_{r}=\frac{\sum_{C_{i}\in\textsc{RCut}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(C_{i})}}{q}

Initialize
m_{r}
many sets
E^{r}_{1},E^{r}_{2}\ldots E^{r}_{m_{r}}
to
\emptyset

\tcp
m_{r}
many extra clusters

Initialize
m_{r}
many variables
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ldots\ell_{m_{r}}
to
q

\tcp
leftover space of extra clusters

Boolean
flag=0

\For

C_{i}\in\textsc{RCut}

\For
j=1
to
m_{r}

Add
k=min(\ell_{j},\textit{$s_{r}$}{(C_{i})})
many red points from
C_{i}
to the cluster
E^{r}_{j}

\ell_{j}=\ell_{j}-k

m_{b}=\frac{\sum_{C_{i}\in\textsc{BCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(C_{i})}}{p}

Initialize
m_{b}
many sets
E^{b}_{1},E^{b}_{2}\ldots E^{b}_{m_{b}}
to
\emptyset

\tcp
m_{b}
many extra clusters

Initialize
m_{b}
many variables
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ldots\ell_{m_{b}}
to
p

\tcp
leftover space of extra clusters

Boolean
flag=0

\For

C_{i}\in\textsc{cut}^{\prime}

\For
j=1
to
m_{b}

Add
k=min(\ell_{j},\textit{$s_{b}$}{(C_{i})})
many blue points from
C_{i}
to the cluster
E^{b}_{j}

\ell_{j}=\ell_{j}-k

\Return
\textsc{RCut}\cup\textsc{BCut}\cup\{E^{r}_{1},E^{r}_{2},\dots,E^{r}_{m_{r}}\}\cup\{E^{b}_{1},E^{b}_{2},\dots,E^{b}_{m_{b}}\}

{algorithm2e}
\DontPrintSemicolon

\text{AlgoforCut-Merge()}(\textsc{RedCut}^{\prime},\textsc{newbcut},\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime})
\KwData
Three clustering as input. For all
D_{i}\in\textsc{newbcut}
we have
p\mid\textit{blue}(D_{i})
, for all
D_{j}\in\textsc{RedCut}^{\prime}
and for all
D_{k}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
we have
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{j})}<q/2
and
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})}<p/2
respectively.

\KwResult
A clustering
\mathcal{Q}
, such that in each cluster
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}
the number of blue points is a multiple of
p
and the number of red points is a multiple of
q
.

RedExtraSum =
\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}^{\prime}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}

m=\frac{\text{RedExtraSum}}{q}

Initialize
m
many sets
E_{1},E_{2}\ldots E_{m}
to
\emptyset

\tcp

m
many extra clusters

Initialize
m
many variables
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ldots\ell_{m}
to
q

\tcp

\ell_{i}
denotes leftover space of extra cluster
E_{i}
.

Boolean
flag=0

\For
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}^{\prime}

\For
j=1
to
m

Add
k=min(\ell_{j},\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})
many red points from
D_{i}
to the cluster
E_{j}

\ell_{j}=\ell_{j}-k

\textsc{RedCut}^{\prime}=\textsc{RedCut}^{\prime}\cup\{E_{1},E_{2},\ldots,E_{n}\}

W_{b}\leftarrow\sum_{D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}}\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})

\For
D_{k}\in(\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime})

Initialize a variable
v_{k}
to
0

\For

a=1
to
W/p

Take the cluster
D_{k}\in(\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime})
for which
\textit{cost}(w_{k,v_{k}})
is minimum

\tcp

Recall
\textit{cost}(w_{k,z})
denotes the cost of cutting the
z
th red subset of size
q
from
D_{k}
.

\While

\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{k}})\neq 0

\For
D_{j}\in\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}

\gamma=\min(\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j}),\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{k}}))

cut
\gamma
many blue points from
D_{k}
and merge to
D_{j}

\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{k}})=\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{k}})-\gamma

\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})=\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})-\gamma

\For
D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}

\If
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})=0

update
\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}=\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}\setminus\{D_{j}\}

\Return

\mathcal{Q}=\textsc{RedCut}^{\prime}\cup\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}

{algorithm2e}

[t]

\DontPrintSemicolon
\textsf{AlgoforMerge-Merge}(\textsc{newrcut},\textsc{newbcut},\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime},\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime})

\KwIn
Clusterings
\textsc{newrcut},\textsc{newbcut},\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime},\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
such that:

•

q\mid|\textit{red}\left(D_{i}\right)|
for all
D_{i}\in\textsc{newrcut}
;
p\mid|\textit{blue}(D_{j})|
for all
D_{j}\in\textsc{newbcut}
.

•

\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{\ell})}>q/2
for
D_{\ell}\in\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
,
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{k})}>p/2
for
D_{k}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
.

•

Clusters in
\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime},\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
are sorted by
(\kappa(D)-\mu(D))
.

\KwOut

Clustering
\mathcal{Q}
with
p\mid|\textit{blue}(Q_{i})|
and
q\mid|\textit{red}\left(Q_{i}\right)|
for all
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}
.

W_{r}\leftarrow\sum_{D\in\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}}\textit{d}(D)

\ForEach

D\in\textsc{newrcut}\cup\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
Set
v_{D}\leftarrow 0

\For

a\leftarrow 1

\KwTo
W_{r}/q

Pick
D_{k}
minimizing
\textit{cost}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},r})

Increment
v_{D_{k}}

\While
\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},r})>0

\ForEach
D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}

\gamma\leftarrow\min(\textit{d}(D_{j}),\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},r}))

Move
\gamma
red points from
D_{k}
to
D_{j}

Update
\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},r})\mathrel{-}=\gamma

Remove any
D_{j}
from
\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}
with
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{j})=0

W_{b}\leftarrow\sum_{D\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}}\textit{d}(D)

\ForEach

D\in\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
Set
v_{D}\leftarrow 0

\For

a\leftarrow 1

\KwTo
W_{b}/p

Pick
D_{k}
minimizing
\textit{cost}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},b})

\While
\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},b})>0

\ForEach
D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}

\gamma\leftarrow\min(\textit{d}(D_{j}),\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},b}))

Move
\gamma
blue points from
D_{k}
to
D_{j}

Update
\textit{size}(w_{k,v_{D_{k}},b})\mathrel{-}=\gamma

Remove any
D_{j}
from
\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}
with
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{j})=0

\Return

\mathcal{Q}=\textsc{newrcut}\cup\textsc{newbcut}\cup\textsc{RedMerge}^{\prime}\cup\textsc{BlueMerge}^{\prime}

We analyze the algorithms for each case separately in
Section

D.3
.
Let
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
be some closest Balance clustering of
\mathcal{D}
.
In
Section

D.2
, we start by introducing notations and useful bounds for
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})
, which we later use for the analysis in
Section

D.3
. The last section
Section

D.4
is dedicated to proving
Theorem

21
, which combines all the cases to come up with the overall approximation.

D.2
Bounds for
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})

We provide some bounds for
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})
. Suppose in
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
a cluster
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
(can be of any type) is split into
t
parts
X_{i,1},X_{i,2},\ldots,X_{i,t}
. Each part
X_{i,j}
is merged with
R_{i,j},B_{i,j}
, the set of red points and blue points outside
D_{i}
. Then, the distance between
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
is

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\left(\sum_{1\leq j<\ell\leq t}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,\ell}|+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\dfrac{|X_{i,j}|(|R_{i,j}|+|B_{i,j}|)}{2}\right).

(54)

We have the following lower bound for
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})
, which is the generalization of
12
.

Lemma 27
.

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\dfrac{\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})}{2}.

Proof:
In
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
, in every cluster
X_{i,j}\cup R_{i,j}\cup B_{i,j}
, the number of red points is a multiple of
q
, and the number of blue points is a multiple of
p
, hence,
|R_{i,j}|\geq\textit{$d_{r}$}(X_{i,j})
, and
|B_{i,j}|\geq\textit{$d_{b}$}(X_{i,j})
. Therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{t}\dfrac{|X_{i,j}|(|R_{i,j}|+|B_{i,j}|)}{2}\geq\sum_{j=1}^{t}\dfrac{\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})}\textit{$d_{r}$}(X_{i,j})}{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\dfrac{\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})}\textit{$d_{b}$}(X_{i,j})}{2}.

It remains to show that
\sum_{j=1}^{t}\dfrac{\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})}\textit{$d_{r}$}(X_{i,j})}{2}\geq\dfrac{\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})}{2}
, and

\sum_{j=1}^{t}\dfrac{\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})}\textit{$d_{b}$}(X_{i,j})}{2}\geq\dfrac{\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})}{2}
. To this end, we apply
Proposition

25
. The proof is complete.

\jmlrQED

Similar to the Cut cost and Merge cost defined in
Appendix

C
we define the following costs:

•

Cut-Cut cost:

\operatorname{\kappa\kappa}(D_{i})=(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}))+\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
.

•

Cut-Merge cost:

\operatorname{\kappa\mu}(D_{i})=\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})
.

•

Merge-Cut cost:

\operatorname{\mu\kappa}(D_{i})=(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})
.

•

Merge-Merge cost:

\operatorname{\mu\mu}(D_{i})=(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|
.

To bound these costs in terms of
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})
, we first rearrange the sum in (
54
) to obtain the following.

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\left(\sum_{j<\ell}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,\ell}|+\textit{$d_{r}$}(X_{i,j})(|X_{i,j}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}},

where
\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}=\sum_{j<\ell}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,\ell}|+\textit{$d_{r}$}(X_{i,j})(|X_{i,j}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(X_{i,j})})
, and

\displaystyle\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\left(\sum_{j<\ell}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,\ell}|+\textit{$d_{b}$}(X_{i,j})(|X_{i,j}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}}\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}},

where
\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}}=\sum_{j<\ell}|X_{i,j}||X_{i,\ell}|+\textit{$d_{b}$}(X_{i,j})(|X_{i,j}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(X_{i,j})})
.

Now note that, utilizing
Lemma

24
, we have

\displaystyle\text{If }\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\leq q/2,\text{ then }\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle>\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\frac{1}{2}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})});

(55)

\displaystyle\text{If }\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}>q/2,\text{ then }\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle>(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\frac{1}{2}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})});

(56)

\displaystyle\text{If }\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}\leq p/2,\text{ then }\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle>\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\frac{1}{2}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})});

(57)

\displaystyle\text{If }\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}>p/2,\text{ then }\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle>(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})+\frac{1}{2}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}).

(58)

Now, we bound those four aforementioned costs in terms of
\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}
and
\textsc{OPT}^{b}{D_{i}}
.

Cost
\operatorname{\kappa\kappa}(D_{i})
for a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}
.

In a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}
,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\leq q/2
and
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(i)}\leq p/2
. Combining (
55
) and (
57
), we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{\kappa\kappa}(D_{i})

\displaystyle=(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}))+\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})+\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\dfrac{\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}^{2}}{2}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})+\dfrac{\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\dfrac{1}{2}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle\>+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})+\dfrac{1}{2}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle=\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}}.

(59)

Cost
\operatorname{\kappa\mu}(D_{i})
for a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}
.

In a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}
,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\leq q/2
and
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(i)}>p/2
. Combining (
55
) and (
58
), we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{\kappa\mu}(D_{i})

\displaystyle=\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle=\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle\leq\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+2\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}}.

(60)

Cost
\operatorname{\mu\kappa}(D_{i})
for a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}
.

In a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}
,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}>q/2
and
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(i)}\leq p/2
. Combining (
56
) and (
57
), we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mu\kappa}(D_{i})

\displaystyle=(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle\leq 2\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}}.

(61)

Cost
\operatorname{\mu\mu}(D_{i})
for a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}
.

In a cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}
,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}>q/2
and
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(i)}>p/2
. Combining (
56
) and (
58
), we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mu\mu}(D_{i})

\displaystyle=(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|

\displaystyle=(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}

\displaystyle\>+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+2\textsc{OPT}^{b}{D_{i}}.

(62)

D.3
Approximation Guarantee

D.3.1
Approximation Guarantee in Cut-Cut Case

In this section, we show that, in the cut-cut case, the clustering output by our algorithm has a distance of at most
6\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})
. In particular, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 28
.

\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q})\leq 6\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})
, where
\mathcal{Q}
is the output of
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
in the cut-cut case (
Section

D.1
of
Section

D.1
).

Observe that the clustering
\mathcal{Q}
output by
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
in the cut-cut case can be described as follows. Consider a cluster
D_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
. If
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}
, then in
\mathcal{Q}
, there will be
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points split from
D_{i}
. If
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
, then in
\mathcal{Q}
, there will be
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})
red points from other clusters merged to
D_{i}
. If there remain red points after merging the split red points from clusters in
RedCut
to fill the deficit of red points in
RedMerge
, clusters of size
q
will be created from these extra points. Similarly, if
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}
, then in
\mathcal{Q}
, there will be
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
blue points split from
D_{i}
. If
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}
, then in
\mathcal{Q}
, there will be
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})
blue points from other clusters merged to
D_{i}
. If there remain blue points after merging the split blue points from clusters in
BlueCut
to fill the deficit of blue points of clusters in
BlueMerge
, clusters of size
p
will be created from these extra points.

Based on these observations, to prove
Lemma

28
, we decompose
\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q})=\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{Q})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})+\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{Q})+\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{Q})
, where each cost is defined as follows.

•

\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{Q})
: the number of pairs
(u,v)
in which in
\mathcal{D}
,
u,v\in D_{i}
, while in
\mathcal{Q}
,
u
is kept in
D_{i}
and
v
is cut from
D_{i}
. In this case,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points of every cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}
will be split from
D_{i}
and be merged to some clusters
D_{j}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
by
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
, or be merged with some red points split from other clusters to create a cluster of size
q
by
AlgoforCut-Cut()
. Similarly,
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
blue points of every cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}
will be split from
D_{i}
and be merged to some clusters
D_{j}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}
by
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
, or be merged with some blue points split from other clusters to create a cluster of size
p
by
AlgoforCut-Cut()
. Hence

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}))+\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}).

(63)

Note that, for
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}
,
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
splits
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points and
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
blue points from
D_{i}
, and thus the cost
(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})
. In (
63
), we add an additional term
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
, which upper bounds to case when
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
may split
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points from
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
blue points. Points among these
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
may also be split from each other. The same situation happens for
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
blue points. That cost is taken into account by
\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{Q})
.

•

\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}):
the number of pairs
(u,v)
in which in
\mathcal{D}
,
u\in D_{i}
and
v\in D_{j}
, while in
\mathcal{Q}
,
v
is split from
D_{j}
and is merged to
D_{i}
, so that
u
and
v
are in the same cluster
D_{i}
. Observe that in the cut-cut case, every cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
will be merged with exactly
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})
red points split from some clusters
D_{j}\in\textsc{RedCut}
. Every cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueMerge}
will be merged with exactly
\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})
blue points split from some clusters
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|+(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})

(64)

•

\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{Q})
: the number of pairs
(u,v)
in which in
\mathcal{D}
,
u\in D_{j}
, and
v\in D_{k}
where
D_{j}
and
D_{k}
are distinct clusters, while in
\mathcal{Q}
,
u
is split from
D_{j}
,
v
is split from
D_{k}
and they are both merged to a cluster
D_{i}
. For each cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}
,
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
merges exactly
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})
red points to it. Note, these
\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})
may come from different clusters from
RedCut
. The same arguments hold for clusters in
BlueMerge
.

Let
RCut
and
BCut
be the input of
AlgoforCut-Cut()
given by
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
. Let
\mathcal{E}^{r}=\{E^{r}_{1},E^{r}_{2},\dots,E^{r}_{m_{r}}\}
and
\mathcal{E}^{b}=\{E^{b}_{1},E^{b}_{2},\dots,E^{b}_{m_{b}}\}
be the set of clusters of size
q
and of size
p
, respectively, formed by
AlgoforCut-Cut()
. For each
E^{r}_{i}
, represent
E^{r}_{i}=Y^{r}_{i,1}\cup Y^{r}_{i,2}\cup\dots\cup Y^{r}_{i,h_{i}}
, where each
Y^{r}_{i,t}
is a set of red points coming from a cluster
D_{i_{t}}\in\textsc{RCut}
. Similarly, for each
E^{b}_{i}
, represent
E^{b}_{i}=Y^{b}_{i,1}\cup Y^{b}_{i,2}\cup\dots\cup Y^{r}_{i,k_{i}}
, where each
Y^{b}_{i,t}
is a set of blue points comming from a cluster
D_{i_{t}}\in\textsc{BCut}
. Employing the same arguments for
17
in the proof of
15
, we have

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{3}(\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}\dfrac{\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i})^{2}}{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}\dfrac{\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\>+

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}\dfrac{(\textit{$d_{r}$}(D_{i})+\textit{$d_{b}$}(D_{i}))^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\>+

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E^{r}_{j}\in\mathcal{E}^{r}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{h_{i}}|Y^{r}_{j,t}|(q-|Y^{r}_{j,t}|)\right)+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{E^{b}_{j}\in\mathcal{E}^{b}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k_{i}}|Y^{b}_{j,t}|(q-|Y^{b}_{j,t}|)\right).

(65)

•

\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{Q})
: the number of pairs
(u,v)
in which
u,v
are among points split from a cluster
D_{i}
, but
u
and
v
are then separated from each other because
u
and
v
are merged to different clusters
D_{j}
and
D_{k}
.

For each cluster
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}
, denote by
R_{i,1},R_{i,2},\dots,R_{i,r_{1}}
the partition of
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}
red points that are split from
D_{i}
, and each of these parts are merged to different clusters in
RedMerge
(by
AlgoforGeneralPQ()
), or be grouped with red points split from other clusters to create clusters of size
q
(by
AlgoforCut-Cut()
). Similarly, let
B_{i,1},B_{i,2},\dots,B_{i,b_{i}}
denote the partition of
\textit{$s_{b}$}{(i)}
blue points split from
D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}
.
Using the same arguments for
16
in the proof of
15
, we have

\displaystyle\textit{cost}_{4}(\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\setminus\textsc{RCut}}\dfrac{\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}^{2}}{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RCut}}\left(\sum_{1\leq j<t\leq r_{i}}|R_{i,j}||R_{i,t}|\right)

\displaystyle\>+

\displaystyle\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BlueCut}\setminus\textsc{BCut}}\dfrac{\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}^{2}}{2}+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{BCut}}\left(\sum_{1\leq j<t\leq b_{i}}|B_{i,j}||B_{i,t}|\right).

(66)

Note that, for
D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}
,
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
red and blue points are split from
D_{i}
. And then, the red part and blue part may be split from each other. The upper bound for this cost
\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}
has been considered in
\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{Q})
.

Claim 19
.

\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{Q})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})\leq 4\,\textit{dist}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{Q}^{*})
.

Proof:

Hence, from
Eq.

63
and
Eq.

64
we have:

\displaystyle\>\>\>\textit{cost}_{1}(\mathcal{Q})+\textit{cost}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-(\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}+\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}))+\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}

\displaystyle\>+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle\>+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})}(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})+(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(|D_{i}|-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle\>+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|+(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})|D_{i}|+(q-\textit{$s_{r}$}{(D_{i})})(p-\textit{$s_{b}$}{(D_{i})})

\displaystyle=\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}\operatorname{\kappa\kappa}(D_{i})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}\operatorname{\kappa\mu}(D_{i})

\displaystyle\>+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}\operatorname{\mu\kappa}(D_{i})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}\operatorname{\mu\mu}(D_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}(\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedCut}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}(\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+2\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}})

\displaystyle\>+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueCut}}(2\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}})+\sum_{D_{i}\in\textsc{RedMerge}\cap\textsc{BlueMerge}}(2\textsc{OPT}^{r}_{D_{i}}+2\textsc{OPT}^{b}_{D_{i}})

(67)
